=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer numberOfUsers representing the total number of users and an array events of size n x 3.
Each events[i] can be either of the following two types:

Message Event: ["MESSAGE", "timestamp_i", "mentions_string_i"]

This event indicates that a set of users was mentioned in a message at timestamp_i.
The mentions_string_i string can contain one of the following tokens:
		
id<number>: where <number> is an integer in range [0,numberOfUsers - 1]. There can be multiple ids separated by a single whitespace and may contain duplicates. This can mention even the offline users.
ALL: mentions all users.
HERE: mentions all online users.




Offline Event: ["OFFLINE", "timestamp_i", "id_i"]

This event indicates that the user id_i had become offline at timestamp_i for 60 time units. The user will automatically be online again at time timestamp_i + 60.



Return an array mentions where mentions[i] represents the number of mentions the user with id i has across all MESSAGE events.
All users are initially online, and if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp.
Note that a user can be mentioned multiple times in a single message event, and each mention should be counted separately.
 
Example 1:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["MESSAGE","10","id1 id0"],["OFFLINE","11","0"],["MESSAGE","71","HERE"]]
Output: [2,2]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id1 and id0 are mentioned. mentions = [1,1]
At timestamp 11, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 71, id0 comes back online and "HERE" is mentioned. mentions = [2,2]

Example 2:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["MESSAGE","10","id1 id0"],["OFFLINE","11","0"],["MESSAGE","12","ALL"]]
Output: [2,2]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id1 and id0 are mentioned. mentions = [1,1]
At timestamp 11, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 12, "ALL" is mentioned. This includes offline users, so both id0 and id1 are mentioned. mentions = [2,2]

Example 3:

Input: numberOfUsers = 2, events = [["OFFLINE","10","0"],["MESSAGE","12","HERE"]]
Output: [0,1]
Explanation:
Initially, all users are online.
At timestamp 10, id0 goes offline.
At timestamp 12, "HERE" is mentioned. Because id0 is still offline, they will not be mentioned. mentions = [0,1]

 
Constraints:

1 <= numberOfUsers <= 100
1 <= events.length <= 100
events[i].length == 3
events[i][0] will be one of MESSAGE or OFFLINE.
1 <= int(events[i][1]) <= 10^5
The number of id<number> mentions in any "MESSAGE" event is between 1 and 100.
0 <= <number> <= numberOfUsers - 1
It is guaranteed that the user id referenced in the OFFLINE event is online at the time the event occurs.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        
```

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, code:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        mentions = [0] * numberOfUsers
        offline_until = [0] * numberOfUsers

        events.sort(key=lambda e: (int(e[1]), 0 if e[0] == "OFFLINE" else 1))

        for typ, ts, data in events:
            t = int(ts)
            if typ == "OFFLINE":
                uid = int(data)
                offline_until[uid] = t + 60
            else:
                # MESSAGE
                if data == "ALL":
                    for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                        mentions[i] += 1
                elif data == "HERE":
                    for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                        if offline_until[i] <= t:
                            mentions[i] += 1
                else:
                    for token in data.split():
                        if token.startswith("id"):
                            uid = int(token[2:])
                            mentions[uid] += 1
                        # If mixed ALL/HERE? not needed. But if token == "ALL" or "HERE", maybe handle? 
        return mentions

Need think if data can be "ALL" and "HERE" with leading/trailing spaces? Spec single whitespace no leading. split handles. If data == "ALL" exactly. If data is "ALL " split gives ["ALL"] but data == "ALL" false; then else branch token "ALL" not starts id, ignore. That would be wrong if trailing spaces. But spec no. Could robustly handle by splitting and processing tokens:
tokens = data.split()
for token in tokens:
    if token == "ALL": ...
    elif token == "HERE": ...
    elif token.startswith("id"): ...
This handles "ALL" with spaces and mixed. But if data == "ALL", tokens ["ALL"], count all. If data == "HERE", count online. This is more robust. Need be careful if token "ALL" and "HERE" mixed with ids? Not spec, but if mixed, counting all plus ids maybe weird. But okay. Use tokens.

Need maybe if data is "id1 id0", tokens. Good.

for token in tokens:
    if token == "ALL":
        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
            mentions[i] += 1
    elif token == "HERE":
        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
            if offline_until[i] <= t:
                mentions[i] += 1
    else:
        # token like id<number>
        uid = int(token[2:])
        mentions[uid] += 1

Now, final code can be:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        mentions = [0] * numberOfUsers
        offline_until = [0] * numberOfUsers

        events.sort(key=lambda e: (int(e[1]), 0 if e[0] == "OFFLINE" else 1))

        for typ, ts, info in events:
            t = int(ts)
            if typ == "OFFLINE":
                uid = int(info)
                offline_until[uid] = t + 60
            else:
                for token in info.split():
                    if token == "ALL":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            mentions[i] += 1
                    elif token == "HERE":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            if offline_until[i] <= t:
                                mentions[i] += 1
                    else:
                        uid = int(token[2:])
                        mentions[uid] += 1

        return mentions

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. But if we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden issue with "id<number>: where <number> is an integer in range [0,numberOfUsers - 1]. There can be multiple ids separated by a single whitespace and may contain duplicates." If mentions string is "id1 id0", split by whitespace. If there are duplicates, count each. Good.

Potential issue: If mentions string is "id1 id0" and user id1 is offline, count. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "ALL" and user offline, count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "OFFLINE" event data "id_i" maybe string "0" and int(data) works. Good.

Potential issue: If `events.sort(key=lambda e: (int(e[1]), 0 if e[0] == "OFFLINE" else 1))` and events is a list of lists, but if e[0] is "MESSAGE" and e[1] is "10", okay. If e[0] is "OFFLINE" and e[1] is "11", okay. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event at timestamp t and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t, but the OFFLINE event is for user A and the MESSAGE event is "HERE". We process OFFLINE first. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp" and "All users are initially online" if an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, but the OFFLINE event is for user A and the MESSAGE event is "ALL". ALL counts all, no difference. Good.

Potential issue: If there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t (online at t). We set offline_until = t+60. Message sees offline. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "timestamp_i + 60" automatic online and "status change processed before message at same timestamp" if there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for a different user. The user is offline for message. If the MESSAGE event is "HERE", not counted. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is after the MESSAGE in original input. Sort puts OFFLINE before MESSAGE. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "It is guaranteed that the user id referenced in the OFFLINE event is online at the time the event occurs." Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop numberOfUsers for each ALL token. If data "ALL" only once. Good.

Now, maybe if there is a MESSAGE event with mentions string "HERE" and user offline_until = t+60, at message t+60 online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t, and the OFFLINE event is for user A, but user A was offline_until = t+60 due to previous OFFLINE? invalid. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "All users are initially online" and "if a user goes offline or comes back online, their status change is processed before handling any message event that occurs at the same timestamp." If a user comes back online at timestamp t+60, and there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for the same user, the user is online at the time OFFLINE event occurs. If we process OFFLINE before MESSAGE, message sees offline. If there is a MESSAGE at timestamp t+60 and no OFFLINE event, message sees online. Good.

Now, maybe if there is an OFFLINE event at timestamp t and a MESSAGE event at timestamp t+60, and an OFFLINE event at timestamp t+60 for same user, but the OFFLINE event at t+60 is processed before the MESSAGE. The user is offline for message. But what if the user's automatic online at t+60 and OFFLINE at t+60 are both status changes, and the message event at t+60 occurs "at timestamp t+60" but maybe after both? The problem says status changes processed before message, so yes.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "events.length <= 100" and "numberOfUsers <= 100" so no performance.

Potential issue: If data is "ALL" and we loop
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countMentions(self, numberOfUsers: int, events: List[List[str]]) -> List[int]:
        mentions = [0] * numberOfUsers
        offline_until = [0] * numberOfUsers

        # Process OFFLINE events before MESSAGE events at the same timestamp.
        events.sort(key=lambda e: (int(e[1]), 0 if e[0] == "OFFLINE" else 1))

        for typ, ts, data in events:
            t = int(ts)

            if typ == "OFFLINE":
                uid = int(data)
                offline_until[uid] = t + 60
            else:
                for token in data.split():
                    if token == "ALL":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            mentions[i] += 1
                    elif token == "HERE":
                        for i in range(numberOfUsers):
                            if offline_until[i] <= t:
                                mentions[i] += 1
                    else:
                        uid = int(token[2:])
                        mentions[uid] += 1

        return mentions
```
